## Decisions (reversible unless noted)
- **Frontend dependency (owner, 2026-11-14):** SheetJS 0.20.3 is vendored under `public/vendor/` for reading Excel files and loaded on demand; it was the only third-party script in the page until 9.4. **pdf.js (owner approved the download, 2026-11-14):** Mozilla `pdfjs-dist` 6.5.438 (Apache-2.0, from the npm registry), legacy build, vendored as `public/vendor/pdf.min.mjs` (sha256 `522ad2c83bc8a0010e7f0cc60d0892b77c8d2bf2c9702dc12288311d94270f42`), `public/vendor/pdf.worker.min.mjs` (sha256 `b4c8353ded0d88d2e2009574a29eb4e0c3ff7e942ecdf51ef5f71566a9667f31`) and `public/vendor/pdfjs.LICENSE.txt`; loaded on first use with `import()`; not in `package.json`. To update: `npm pack pdfjs-dist@<version>` in a temp folder, copy `legacy/build/pdf.min.mjs`, `legacy/build/pdf.worker.min.mjs` and `LICENSE`, record version and sha256 here, re-run the PDF checks of 9.4. The character-map and font folders of the package are not vendored (a PDF that needs them may give no text for those fonts). To update it, download the new `xlsx.full.min.js` from `cdn.sheetjs.com`, record version and sha256 here, and re-run an import of an `.xls` and an `.xlsx`.
- **Stack:** Node >=22.5 (ESM) + Express 5; built-in `node:sqlite` (file under `data/`, git-ignored); `node:test`. No build step, no frontend framework. Dependencies: `express`, `@anthropic-ai/sdk` (added in 4.2 — the provider's official SDK instead of hand-written HTTP).
- **AI path:** only `server/ai.js` talks to the model; only `buildContext` (`server/director.js`) supplies company material for a prompt.
- **Frontend:** `public/index.html` is the live single-file vanilla-JS app (EN/AR + RTL). Root `BrandPilot.html` is the untouched reference. Patch in place; never rewrite or redesign. Lines are very long — locate code with `grep -n -o 'pattern.\{0,200\}'`, never read the file whole. Patch with exact-string edits (shell heredocs mangle its regex backslashes).
- **Frontend state:** global `S = { v, lang, active, companies:{id→obj}, ui, ai }`; `C()` = active company; every `render()` calls `save()`, which schedules `syncNow()` — it diffs `S.companies` against what was last sent (new → `POST` + re-key to server id, removed → `DELETE`, changed → `PUT .../state`). Code that mutates a company and calls `R()` is persisted automatically. `localStorage` holds only `lang`, `active`, `ui`, `ai`.
- **Isolation rule:** every company-scoped route is `/api/companies/:id/...` behind `requireCompany` (404 for non-members); queries filter by `req.company.id`, never by an id from the body.
- **API conventions:** JSON errors `{ error: code }` — 400 invalid input, 401, 403, 404, 409, 413 `too_large`, 429 `too_many_attempts`. State-changing routes are POST/PUT/DELETE with JSON bodies (CSRF relies on SameSite=Lax + JSON-only). Tests call `createApp({ db: openDb(':memory:') })`; `createApp` also takes `authLimits`, `fetchUrl`, `fetchLimit`, `ai`, `askLimit`.
- **Mirror pattern (use it again in Phase 3):** the prototype keeps working client-side inside the state blob; on every `PUT .../state` the server validates and copies the parts it must be able to trust into company-scoped tables in the same transaction. Malformed items are skipped and counted, never fatal. Server features read the tables, never the blob.
- **Facts read path:** server code reads facts only through `approvedFacts(db, companyId)`.
- **Data read path:** server code reads imported rows only through `dataRows(db, companyId, kind)` (or `createData(db).rows`), never from the blob. The client and server can disagree: the browser keeps rows the server refused (for example negative numbers) until 3.6 tightens the client. KPIs for server code come only from `metrics(db, companyId, range)`. Audit items for server code come only from `audit(db, companyId)`. Campaigns for server code come only from `campaigns(db, companyId)` (or `createCampaigns(db).get`).
- **Data today (prototype, client-side):** `importData(c,text,origin,fname,kind)` parses CSV/JSON into `c.data = { meta[], ga4[], gsc[], leads[] }` using schemas in `SCH` (required / optional / numeric columns), records `c.imports[]`; `audit(c)` builds recommendations with `ev` arrays; `learn(c)`, `campResult`. The rows are mirrored on the server (3.2); KPIs and audit are still computed only in the browser.
- A new account starts with one empty company; no synthetic seed. Synthetic demo material loads only through the explicit, labeled "load demo" action.
- **Known limits:** last write wins across tabs/devices; company state over 5 MB fails to save with a visible error; rate limiters are in-memory per process (login keys on `req.ip` — set Express `trust proxy` behind a reverse proxy); companies saved before 2.2 have no mirror rows until their next save; the frontend has no automated tests — frontend changes are verified by browser runs only; website reading fetches one page, runs no scripts, ignores robots.txt; the prototype's audit / director / campaign screens still compute in the browser from the blob (the "only approved facts" rule is enforced on the server read path, which those screens do not use yet).
- **Prototype quirks:** fixed in 6.10 (focus after basics fields, demo guide on non-demo companies) and 6.7 (`/favicon.ico`). No UI path creates a demo company any more (see 3.1 h) — add a labeled "create demo company" action only if the owner wants demos; until then the demo guide never shows.

Sign-in changed to what the MCP server publishes (read without credentials on 2026-11-15: `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/connect/v1/mcp` → authorization server `www.canva.com`): token URL `https://api.canva.com/auth/v1/oauth/token` (override `CANVA_TOKEN_URL`), `resource` parameter, scope `brandkit:read` added. `server/canvaStudio.js`: tools and their input schemas are read from `tools/list` per user — only the documented tool NAMES are fixed (`generate-image`, `get-generate-image-job`, `generate-design`, `create-design-from-candidate`, `get-design`, `export-design`); `fitArgs` maps BrandPilot's values onto whatever property names the schema has and refuses when a required one cannot be filled; `readResult` reads answers tolerantly (job id, proposals, design id, edit / view link, picture links, credit note); `canvaBrief` builds the text from the validated campaign mirror + approved colours + the user's style note (a picture: no text, no logo; a design: the post's own wording, no added claims, no drawn logo). The approved logo is uploaded as a file (`POST /rest/v1/asset-uploads`, once per logo and user) and passed only when the design tool's schema takes assets. **9.x series (owner, 2026-11-14) — order: 9.1, 9.17, 9.2 … 9.9, then 9.14, 9.15, 9.16; 9.10–9.13 only after the owner approves them; apart from 9.15 (needs the owner's Facebook and Instagram export files in `data/`) they need no owner input** (the AI key is set). Full context and the prompt for each task: `TASK_PROMPTS.md`. Decisions taken while writing them, all reversible — tell the owner when the task starts: PDFs are read through their text layer in the browser, not sent to the model (9.4), so scanned PDFs are not read; the AI analysis runs once per source as part of the normal analysis (9.6); a website scan reads at most 8 pages (9.7); website colours / logo / font are pending findings until approved (9.9); existing campaigns are not restyled or rewritten automatically (9.9, 9.11). The owner's point "channel checkboxes don't work" was diagnosed from the code only, not in a browser.

## Next atomic task

**Newest — 15: Analytics channel comparison (first handed off as "12" / "12b"; renumbered because dev's 12–14 are the creative-studio tasks) (2026-11-16, Claude Opus 5.5; owner's brief "unified, cross-platform marketing intelligence dashboard").** Committed as e49e79f on `feat/12-analytics-compare` (not merged into `dev`, not pushed as far as this checkout shows).
- **Math** — `public/compare.js` (classic script, global `BPCompare`, pure, no DOM, no wording). `measure` / `rank` / `coverage` / `findings` / `compare(data, cur, prev)`. Honesty rules: a column the data lacks is `null` ("Not measured"), never 0; leads are counted by the CRM `source` the team typed (`leadCol`: names Facebook / Instagram / LinkedIn / SEO-"organic search"; **"Google" stays unassigned** — organic or ads); organic-search leads are `null` unless some source names it; GA4 sessions per platform only when the GA4 channel names the platform, else one "all social" pool (`ga4Col`); a rate is ranked only above `MIN` (1,000 reach, 100 sessions, 5 leads, 10 leads for qualified share, 30 clicks) in ≥ 2 channels and with a ≥ 10 % lead (`EDGE`).
- **Screen** — `V.analytics` opens on tab `cmp` "Compare channels" (`cxView` and helpers `cxSource` / `cxText` / `cxCell` / `cxRow` just above `V.analytics`; CSS `.cx-*` at the end of the style block). Order: same-dates note → findings (Observed fact / Interpretation / Risk, each with its caveat) → side-by-side table grouped Recorded outcomes / Website visits (GA4) / Spend / Reach & engagement / Search only, each row labeled Comparable / Compare with care / Context only / No equivalent, ★ best, "small sample", change vs previous period, rate bars → "What this comparison cannot tell yet" → Sources and freshness (Connected · live sync only when the Meta / Google status says connected AND rows came from a sync; files are "Imported file") → unassigned lead sources. Old tabs (Overview, platforms, GA4, search, LinkedIn) unchanged.
- **Actions** — `A.cmpAsk(i)` asks the Director the finding as a question with the period; `A.cmpCamp(k)` makes a Studio draft (`newCampaign`, rec id `compare-<channel>`, channels = that platform, evidence = that channel's findings).
- **Director** — `server/channels.js` runs the same `public/compare.js` in `node:vm` (`channelSummary(data, range, currency)` with `CHANNEL_RULES`); `buildContext(db, id, { range })` adds `context.channels` (default range '28'; the page sends `S.ui.range` with `/director/ask`; `GET .../director/context?range=`). New citation type `channel` with id `<channel>.<metric>`, checked in `lookup`; the page labels it "Channel comparison". Before this, the real AI answered "the context does not include the platform lead counts".
- **15b — ranking explanations (owner's report: "Facebook 3.68 % marked ★ best while Instagram has 15.9 % and is marked small sample").** The math was right (Instagram's reach was under the 1,000 minimum); the screen did not say so. Now: `RANK[k].need` lists the minimum per metric as `[field, min]`; `BPCompare.short(k, o)` returns the first unmet one `{ f, have, min }`; `rank()` also returns `info[k]` = `{ status: 'ranked' | 'close' | 'few', top, run, edge, obs (highest observed of ALL channels), out (not ranked, each with f / have / min), eligible }`, exposed as `compare().rank`. Findings: a `best` finding carries `above` (the better-looking unranked channel); new finding `unranked` (cls `hyp`, badge "Too little data to rank", no campaign button) whenever the highest observed value is not ranked. Page: `cxCell` markers (`.cx-tag` `.cx-tb` / `.cx-th` / `.cx-tx`), `cxRankNote` (the "Why:" row `tr.cx-n`), `CXW` (visible comparability reason), `cxNeed` / `cxWhyOut` (reason wording), a legend `.cx-leg` above the table; the footnote lists the minimums from `BPCompare.MIN` and says they are BrandPilot rules, not a statistical test. Source cards say "Best eligible on" / "Lowest eligible on". Server: `channelSummary().ranking` (`bestEligible`, `highestObserved`, `notRanked` with reason) + a rule telling the model it may call only `bestEligible` best and must mention a better-looking unranked channel.
- **Not done / next**: (a) a "Compare campaigns" view by `campaign` tag across platforms is still only the old Overview table; (b) the Markdown / printable report has no channel table; (c) LinkedIn live sync does not exist (file import only); (d) the rules-engine Director (no AI) does not use `channels`; (e) "Google" lead source could be split if the owner records "Google Ads" vs "SEO" — ask before adding mappings.
- **Next single task**: 15c — add the channel table to the report (`server/report.js`, reuse `channelSummary`). Success: report and screen show the same per-channel numbers for the same period.

**Also new — 12 (creative studio, not the analytics task): colours, logo position, Canva design as the whole post (2026-11-16, Fable 5.2; six requests from the owner after trying task 11 with the real Canva account). Committed and pushed to `dev` on 2026-11-16. Git-ignored helper: `data/check-12-steps.mjs`.**
- **Any colour**: a design colour is a brand role or `#RRGGBB` (`dcol`). The Look tab shows swatches for background, text, second background (gradient) and button: brand colours, a wider palette (`MODERN`), and a colour input for any colour (`swPick`, `A.dcol`). Brand QA's contrast check uses the same colours. Company templates keep them.
- **Logo position**: `design.logo` = `t|b` + `s|c|e` (start = the side the text starts on) or `off`; default top, far side (`logoXY`, `logoPick`, `A.lpos`). The picker is physical (left / centre / right), converted per post language. The text block keeps clear of a bottom logo. Changing the look keeps the position (`keepD`).
- **No company-name text beside a logo**: the name is written only when the company has no logo at all (`nameOn`).
- **Logo plate**: a light logo that would not stand out gets a dark plate, a dark one a white plate (`logoMark`, `lum` from `logoFor`).
- **Canva logo**: with the logo attached as an asset, every real proposal still carried a redrawn, wrong logo (owner's screenshots). The brief now asks Canva for NO logo or wordmark and a clear top (`canvaBrief`); the logo is still uploaded to the user's Canva uploads but no longer handed to the generator (`assets` removed from the call). **Not verified against real Canva — the first thing to check.**
- **A Canva design replaces the artwork**: "Use in this post" on a design sets `design.fill = 'full'` — `bannerSVG` draws the design as the whole post with only the real logo on top (average colour of the picture, `imgAvg`, decides the plate). A Canva picture (no text) still sits beside the wording. The Picture tab has "How the picture is used"; the Words tab says when the wording is not drawn.
- **One design per post**: "Generate a design for every post (N)" (`A.cvAll`) runs one generation per post in turn, after a confirm; it stops at the first refusal from Canva (plan, credits, limit — the server allows 12 generations per 10 minutes). Posts with waiting proposals carry a "Canva" badge in the list.
- **13 — PNG download (same day, requested after 12)**: a post has two buttons, "Download PNG" and "Download SVG" (other assets keep one "Download"). PNG = the post drawn at the format shown (1080×1080, 1080×1350, 1080×1920 or 1200×628) on a canvas in the browser (`A.aPng`), named `<slug>-<lang>-post<n>-<channel>-<W>x<H>[-DRAFT].png`; it also works for a post that is a whole Canva design. Checked: `data/check-13-steps.mjs` 6/6 (real PNG signature, the four sizes, the logo is in the pixels, SVG unchanged) and a PNG line in `data/check-12-steps.mjs` (22/22). The Export page's ZIP still holds SVGs only.
